# Roadmap — SpecPilot / test-automation-agent
**Maturity:** MEDIUM (backend + frontend exist; Fly deploy). **Stopping point:** a solid test-run + flake-management product; don't over-expand into a full observability suite.

## P1 — core test-run product (next ~2 quarters)
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Test-run summaries, status, retry/rerun decisions, flake ratios (pure logic + endpoints) {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Run dashboard UI: status colors, durations, pass/flake surfaces {cat: web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [x] [P1] [resolved 2026-09-09] Fix the Fly deploy (specpilot routes) — VERIFIED live: https://specpilot.fly.dev/health -> 200 and / -> 200 with a real API response ({"name":"Test Automation Agent API",...}). This was already fixed (see shared/CLAUDE.md's 2026-09-01 full re-verification) but the roadmap never got updated, so it kept showing as the #1 blocker with nothing left to actually research. {cat: infra; size: S; research: web}
## P2 — integrations (~q3-4)
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] CI integrations (GitHub Actions/Jenkins) result ingestion — researched 2026-09-09: GitHub needs a GitHub App (checks:write scope — plain OAuth apps/PATs can't create check runs) that POSTs to /repos/{owner}/{repo}/check-runs on push/PR and updates it to completed+conclusion when the SpecPilot run finishes (docs.github.com "Building CI checks with a GitHub App"). Jenkins side: the Outbound Webhook plugin (NOT Generic Webhook Trigger, which triggers INTO Jenkins, the wrong direction) sends build-event payloads OUT to an external endpoint. Decomposes into: (1) a generic `POST /api/ci/webhook` ingestion endpoint accepting {provider, repo, commit_sha, status, run_id}, (2) a GitHub-specific outbound path using the Checks API to report SpecPilot's own run status back onto the PR, (3) a docs page with copy-paste GitHub Actions YAML + Jenkins plugin config. {cat: backend; size: M; research: web; sources: docs.github.com/en/free-pro-team@latest/developers/apps/creating-ci-tests-with-the-checks-api, plugins.jenkins.io/outbound-webhook}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Knowledge-base / test-selection helpers {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: no; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Flake-state Slack/webhook alerts — When a test's rolling flake ratio crosses a configured threshold (or a previously-stable test starts failing intermittently), push a message to a Slack/Discord/generic webhook URL configured per-project, and post a follow-up when it stabilizes. This reuses the flake-ratio computation and CI ingestion events already planned (GitHub Checks/Jenkins webhook) as the trigger source — no new data source, just a notification sink off existing state transitions. Stays test-run scoped because it fires on flake-ratio state changes only, not generic error/log alerting; it's the single highest-leverage feature for getting a first team to check the tool daily instead of only visiting the dashboard. {cat: backend+web; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none — completed 2026-09-10, sources: Trunk Flaky Tests Slack integration (trunk.io/flaky-tests), BuildPulse per-repo flake notifications (docs.buildpulse.io)}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] "Fix-first" ranking view — Add a dashboard view/API endpoint that ranks tests by (flake-rate × run-frequency) so a team can see which handful of flaky tests are costing the most CI time/trust, rather than an undifferentiated flaky-tests list. Pure derived-data feature computed entirely from run records and flake ratios already ingested via the CI integrations; no new collection surface. Stays scoped to test-run triage (a prioritized to-do list for existing flake data) rather than sliding into general analytics/observability. {cat: backend+web; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none — completed 2026-09-10, sources: CircleCI Test Insights "most failed/flakiest tests" ranking (circleci.com/docs/guides/insights/insights-tests), Buildkite Test Analytics slowest/least-reliable dashboard}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Per-test flake-rate trend chart — On each test's detail page, render a time-series/sparkline of its flake ratio and pass/fail history over recent runs (last N days or last N runs), so a team can see whether a test is getting better, worse, or newly-flaky at a glance instead of only a point-in-time ratio. Requires storing/aggregating flake ratio per run-window rather than just a rolling scalar, but the underlying run records already come from the planned CI ingestion pipeline — this is a read-model/charting addition, not a new data source. Stays in scope as a visualization of test-run pass/fail history, not logs or infra metrics. {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none — completed 2026-09-10, sources: Buildkite Test Analytics historical/graph trend views (buildkite.com/resources/blog/fixing-flaky-tests), Datadog Flaky Test Management history-across-runs (docs.datadoghq.com/tests/flaky_management)}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] PR-comment run summary — Post a single upserted PR comment (via the same GitHub App/token used for the planned Checks API integration) summarizing the run: pass/fail counts, which tests are newly flaky, and a link to the run's dashboard page — distinct from the Checks API status badge, since a comment is scannable in the PR conversation without opening a details link. Reuses the existing GitHub integration's auth and webhook events; only adds a comment-upsert call keyed on PR number. Stays scoped to summarizing this repo's test-run results, not general CI/build commentary. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none — completed 2026-09-10, sources: BuildPulse PR comments/blocking on new flakes (buildpulse.io/products/flaky-tests), general flaky-test PR-comment pattern across CircleCI/Buildkite integrations}
## P2 — flake/CI wiring gaps found in code audit (2026-09-11: several past decompositions landed only pure-logic utils that were never wired into a real code path — verified via grep, zero callers outside their own tests)
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Rerun-failed-only endpoint — Add `POST /api/test-runs/{run_id}/rerun-failed` (backend/app/routers/test_runs.py) that creates a new run scoped to only the failed test names from a prior run instead of requiring a full new YAML test plan. Wires up backend/app/utils/rerun_budget.py (can_rerun/remaining) and backend/app/utils/retry_decision.py (should_retry) — both fully unit-tested pure helpers with zero callers anywhere in backend/app/services or routers today — to enforce a per-run rerun budget in backend/app/services/execution_engine.py. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Auto-release stale quarantines — backend/app/services/flake_detection.py's record_result() only ever sets `quarantined = True` (the code comment literally says "sticky: never auto-unquarantines"), so a test that stabilizes stays on the fix-first list forever. Extend backend/app/utils/quarantine_policy.py with a `should_release(consecutive_passes, release_after)` pure function, add a `flake_quarantine_release_after` setting, and call it from record_result() to auto-clear `quarantined` after N consecutive passes since quarantine. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Wire PR-comment upsert into the GitHub API — backend/app/utils/pr_comment_body.py (build_pr_comment_body) and backend/app/utils/pr_comment_marker.py (get_pr_comment_marker) are tested pure helpers that are never imported outside their own test files — no code path actually posts a PR comment today. Add `post_or_update_pr_comment()` to backend/app/services/github_ci.py that GETs `/repos/{repo}/issues/{pr_number}/comments`, finds an existing comment containing the marker, and PATCHes it (or POSTs new) with build_pr_comment_body() output; call it from backend/app/services/execution_engine.py next to the existing update_check_run() call sites (~line 398-454) for CI-linked runs. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Visual-regression diff flagging — backend/app/utils/visual_diff.py (diff_ratio, is_visual_regression) has zero callers, but test runs already capture per-step screenshots to artifact storage (backend/app/services/execution_engine.py ~line 109-115 via artifact_storage.save_base64). Add a comparison step that, when a test has a screenshot from both the current run and the immediately-preceding run of the same test_plan_name, computes a pixel-diff ratio (Pillow) and stores an `is_visual_regression` flag on the TestResult; surface a "visual regression" badge on frontend/src/pages/TestRunDetailPage.vue via frontend/src/stores/testRuns.js. {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Remove dead fix-first stub router — backend/app/routers/fix_first.py is a leftover stub (`APIRouter()` with no prefix, no auth) defining `GET /fix-first` that returns a hardcoded `{"status": "success"}`; it's dead scaffolding from an earlier decomposition pass since the real, fully-implemented fix-first ranking feature already lives at `GET /api/flakes/fix-first` in backend/app/routers/flakes.py. Delete backend/app/routers/fix_first.py and its import/include_router call in backend/app/main.py (lines 12 and 71) so there's no unauthenticated fake endpoint left in the API surface. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## P2 — flake/CI wiring gaps found in code audit round 2 (2026-09-13: re-ran the zero-caller grep audit across backend/app/utils; the 2026-09-11 batch is now wired except visual_diff.py (already queued below as "Visual-regression diff flagging"). Found a much larger set of still-orphaned pure helpers than expected — picking the ones directly on the test-run/flake-management stopping point, not the full list.)
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Surface overall suite health badge on Flake Dashboard — backend/app/utils/suite_health.py's `health(pass_rate, flake_rate)` is a fully-tested pure classifier (returns healthy/flaky/degraded) with zero callers anywhere in backend/app (verified via grep). GET /api/flakes/analysis (backend/app/routers/flakes.py) already computes `overall_pass_rate` and `avg_flake_score` for the customer but doesn't classify them. Add an `overall_health` field to that endpoint's response using `health()`, fetch it in frontend/src/stores/flakes.js, and render it on frontend/src/pages/FlakeDashboardPage.vue's header using the also-orphaned frontend/src/utils/suiteBadge.ts (zero callers, already has healthy/flaky/degraded label+color mapping) — today the dashboard shows a per-test table with no aggregate health signal at all. {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Wire tiered flake-severity badges into the Flake Dashboard — backend/app/utils/flake_indicator.py's `get_flake_badge(flake_score)` (5-tier Stable/Low/Medium/High/Critical label+color, fully unit-tested) has zero callers anywhere in backend/app. routers/flakes.py's `_serialize()` (~line 19) currently only returns the raw `flake_score` float. Add a `badge` field ({label, color}) to `_serialize()` via `get_flake_badge()`, and update frontend/src/pages/FlakeDashboardPage.vue to render `flake.badge.label`/`flake.badge.color` in place of its own hardcoded 3-tier `severityClass()` function (~line 95). {cat: backend+web; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Consolidate duplicate status-color helpers and wire flaky coloring into the run list — three fully-tested, near-duplicate status-to-color implementations exist with zero callers outside their own tests: backend/app/utils/status_color.py, frontend/src/utils/statusColor.ts, and frontend/src/utils/runStatusColor.ts (the only one of the three that also handles a `'flaky'` status). frontend/src/pages/TestRunsPage.vue's `getStatusBadge()` (~line 100) and TestRunDetailPage.vue instead hardcode their own Tailwind class maps that don't have a 'flaky' case at all. Wire frontend/src/utils/runStatusColor.ts into both pages' status badges (apply its hex color as an inline `background-color` style alongside the existing Tailwind text classes), then delete the now-fully-redundant frontend/src/utils/statusColor.ts and backend/app/utils/status_color.py plus their dedicated test files. {cat: web; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Remove dead duplicate select_by_tag helper — backend/app/utils/test_selection.py's `select_by_tag(tests, tag)` is a fully-tested pure function with zero callers anywhere in backend/app; it's a strict subset of the already-wired `select_tests(tests, include_tags=[tag])` (used in routers/test_plans.py and services/execution_engine.py). Delete `select_by_tag` and its dedicated test to remove the confusing duplicate surface, following the same precedent as the earlier dead `fix_first` router removal. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Add unit tests for the flakes Pinia store — frontend/src/stores/flakes.js (fetchFlakes, setQuarantine, loading/error state) has zero test coverage today; frontend/src/stores/__tests__/testRuns.test.js is the only existing store test in the repo and establishes the mocked-axios + Pinia setup pattern to follow. Add frontend/src/stores/__tests__/flakes.test.js covering: a successful fetch populates `flakes`, a fetch error sets `error` and leaves data empty, and `setQuarantine` both updates the matching entry in place on success and surfaces the API error message on failure. {cat: test; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## P2/P3 — round 3 audit (2026-09-14: fleet ran dry — 0 ready, 0 backlog. Re-ran the zero-caller grep audit across backend/app/utils and backend/app/agents; found two live wiring gaps plus four dead/duplicate/inconsistent helpers not covered by the round-1/round-2 batches above.)
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Wire flake-trend direction into the per-test trend endpoint — `GET /api/flakes/{test_name}/trend` (backend/app/routers/flakes.py, function `get_test_trend`) returns the raw pass/fail `history` list (list[bool], see backend/app/models/test_run.py's `history = Column(JSON, ...)` ~line 158) but no trend direction. backend/app/utils/flake_trend.py's `trend_direction(scores: list[float])` has zero callers anywhere in backend/app (verified via grep). backend/app/utils/flake_score.py's `compute_flake_score(history: list[bool]) -> float` is already imported and used in backend/app/services/flake_detection.py. In `get_test_trend`, split `stat.history` into earlier/recent halves, run `compute_flake_score()` on each half to get a 2-value `[earlier_score, recent_score]` list, pass it to `trend_direction()`, and add a `"trend"` field ("improving"/"worsening"/"stable") to the response dict. {cat:backend; size:S; multifile:no; research:none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Wire selector_scoring.score_selector into the optimizer's ranking — backend/app/utils/selector_scoring.py's `score_selector(success_rate, robustness)` (fully unit-tested composite scorer that adds a robustness bonus on top of success_rate) has zero callers anywhere in backend/app (verified via grep). backend/app/agents/optimizer.py's `SelectorOptimizer.find_element()` (~line 90) currently sets `"confidence": success_rate` directly, using `robustness` only as a separate sort tie-breaker via the hardcoded `_ROBUSTNESS_RANK` dict — so two selectors with identical success_rate but different robustness get the same confidence score. Replace that line with `"confidence": score_selector(success_rate, strategy.get("robustness", "unknown"))` so stronger (id/data-testid) selectors actually score higher, not just tie-break. Existing tests backend/tests/test_optimizer_find_element.py and test_optimizer_dedup.py assert membership/counts, not exact confidence values, so this is a safe swap. {cat:backend; size:S; multifile:no; research:none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Remove dead duplicate rbac.py — backend/app/utils/rbac.py's `has_permission`/`role_rank` (fully tested, zero callers anywhere in backend/app per grep) duplicate the real, wired-in permission check: backend/app/models/tenancy.py's `role_at_least()` (used at backend/app/routers/orgs.py line 99), backed by `ROLE_RANK = {"member": 0, "admin": 1, "owner": 2}`. rbac.py invents an incompatible 4-tier `_ROLE_RANKS = {"owner": 4, "admin": 3, "member": 2, "viewer": 1}` — a "viewer" role that doesn't exist anywhere else in the codebase (models/tenancy.py's `VALID_ROLES` is only member/admin/owner). Delete backend/app/utils/rbac.py and backend/tests/test_rbac.py to remove the confusing, inconsistent dead duplicate. {cat:backend; size:S; multifile:no; research:none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Remove dead org_scoping.py — backend/app/utils/org_scoping.py's `can_access`/`require_owner` (fully tested, zero callers anywhere in backend/app per grep) implement a plain `owner_id == requester_id` ownership check that was never adopted. The actual authorization pattern used throughout the codebase is customer_id-scoped, e.g. backend/app/routers/webhooks.py's `_owned_endpoint()` (~line 82) does `if endpoint.customer_id != customer_id: raise HTTPException(403, ...)` directly — a different shape than org_scoping's two-arbitrary-string-IDs signature. Delete backend/app/utils/org_scoping.py and backend/tests/test_org_scoping.py. {cat:backend; size:S; multifile:no; research:none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Delete duplicate get_relevant_tests method in knowledge_base.py — backend/app/services/knowledge_base.py defines `async def get_relevant_tests(self, feature_name)` TWICE with byte-identical bodies (verified via grep+read: definitions at line 50 and line 244, both calling `query_knowledge_base(feature_name, top_k=10)` and returning the same test_ids list), so the second silently shadows the first at class-definition time — dead, confusing duplication in a class where every other method is defined once. Delete the second (~line 244) definition. {cat:backend; size:S; multifile:no; research:none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Remove dead duplicate duration_format.py — backend/app/utils/duration_format.py's `format_duration(ms)` (fully tested, zero callers anywhere in backend/app per grep) duplicates backend/app/utils/duration.py's `format_duration_ms(ms)`, which is the real, wired-in formatter (imported and called at backend/app/routers/test_runs.py line 18 and ~320 to populate the `duration_human` field). The two disagree on edge cases — duration_format.py raises `ValueError` on negative input, duration.py clamps to `"0ms"` — so keeping both risks a future edit picking the wrong one. Delete backend/app/utils/duration_format.py and backend/tests/test_duration_format.py. {cat:backend; size:S; multifile:no; research:none}

## Stopping point
Test-run tracking + flake detection + CI ingestion + a clean dashboard = COMPLETE. Maintain.

## P2/P3 — round 4 audit (2026-09-14: fleet ran dry again — 0 ready, 0 backlog. Re-ran zero-caller grep across the full backend/app/utils tree with a stricter non-test-dir grep; the round-1/2/3 batches undercounted because several test files use a test_utils_X.py naming pattern that a loose grep skipped. Found one real endpoint bug, one inline-duplicate-vs-tested-util gap, and three duplicate-implementation clusters — including a genuine three-way secret-masking divergence.)
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Fix test-plan selection endpoint that always returns empty — `POST /api/test-plans/{plan_id}/select` (backend/app/routers/test_plans.py line 75, `select_tests` handler) calls `tps.get_relevant_tests(plan_id, body.query)` which is backend/app/services/test_plan_selection.py's `get_relevant_tests(plan_id, query)` — a stub that unconditionally `return []` (comment: "plan storage is not yet implemented"). Meanwhile backend/app/services/knowledge_base.py's `KnowledgeBaseService.get_relevant_tests(feature_name)` (line 85) already does real work via `query_knowledge_base()` (backend/app/utils/kb_query.py) and is instantiated elsewhere as `KnowledgeBaseService(db, context.customer_id)` (backend/app/services/execution_engine.py line 420). Change test_plan_selection.get_relevant_tests to accept db+customer_id, delegate to KnowledgeBaseService.get_relevant_tests(query), and update the router call site (add `db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_db)` like other routers, e.g. backend/app/routers/flakes.py) so the endpoint returns real matches instead of always `[]`. {cat:backend; size:M; multifile:yes; research:none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Wire step_histogram helpers into orchestrator's difficulty heuristic — backend/app/agents/orchestrator.py's `_classify_difficulty_heuristic` (line 53) hand-rolls `action_counts = Counter(actions)` (line 60) and `has_wait = any(a.startswith("wait_") for a in actions)` (line 63) inline, duplicating the already-written, fully-tested backend/app/utils/step_histogram.py functions `action_counts(steps)` and `has_wait(steps)` (verified zero callers anywhere in backend/app via grep — only its own test file references them). Replace the two inline lines with `from app.utils.step_histogram import action_counts as _action_counts, has_wait as _has_wait` and call them directly on `steps` (they already do their own `.get("action")`/`.lower()` handling), removing the duplicate logic and giving step_histogram.py real production callers. {cat:backend; size:S; multifile:no; research:none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Remove dead duplicate webhook event-matcher — backend/app/utils/event_pattern.py's `event_matches(event, subscriptions)` has zero callers anywhere in backend/app (verified via grep; only backend/tests/test_event_pattern.py references it). The real, wired implementation is backend/app/services/notification_dispatcher.py's own `event_matches(event, subscribed)` (defined line 32, called line 146 to filter WebhookEndpoint subscriptions before delivery) — which is a superset (also supports the bare `"*"` catch-all that event_pattern.py's version doesn't handle). Delete backend/app/utils/event_pattern.py and backend/tests/test_event_pattern.py to remove the confusing, weaker duplicate. {cat:backend; size:S; multifile:no; research:none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Remove dead duplicate result-summarization helpers — backend/app/utils/allure_summary.py's `summarize(results)` (passed/failed/broken/skipped counts) and backend/app/utils/run_summary.py's `summarize()`/`all_green()`/`build_run_summary()` all have zero callers anywhere in backend/app (verified via grep; only their own test files — backend/tests/test_allure_summary.py, backend/tests/test_run_summary.py — reference them). Both duplicate functionality already wired in production: backend/app/utils/results_summary.py's `summarize_results()` (used at backend/app/routers/public_api.py line 75) and backend/app/utils/dashboard_metrics.py's `calculate_dashboard_summary()` (used at backend/app/routers/test_runs.py line 211). Delete allure_summary.py, run_summary.py, and their two test files. {cat:backend; size:S; multifile:yes; research:none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Remove three-way duplicate/conflicting secret-masking helpers — backend/app/utils/mask.py's `mask_secret(value, keep=4)`, backend/app/utils/mask_secret.py's `mask_secret(value, visible=4)`, and backend/app/utils/redact.py's `redact_secret(value, keep=4)` (line 44) are three independently-written implementations of "mask a secret string" with zero callers anywhere in backend/app (verified via grep — each is referenced only by its own test: test_mask.py, test_mask_secret.py/test_utils_mask_secret.py, test_utils_redact.py). They actively disagree on behavior: mask.py's version returns a short value UNMASKED when `len(value) <= keep`, while redact.py's `redact_secret` FULLY MASKS a value of the same length in the same situation — a real correctness/security divergence that risks picking the wrong one later. mask_secret.py's version masks the middle instead of the prefix (different semantic entirely) and its own docstring already cross-references redact.redact_secret, showing the duplication was known and not cleaned up. Delete backend/app/utils/mask.py and backend/app/utils/mask_secret.py entirely (plus test_mask.py, test_mask_secret.py, test_utils_mask_secret.py); remove just the `redact_secret` function (and its dedicated test cases) from backend/app/utils/redact.py and backend/tests/test_utils_redact.py, keeping that file's unrelated `redact_secrets()`/`redact_all()` text-scrubbing functions intact. {cat:backend; size:S; multifile:yes; research:none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Add p50/p95 duration percentiles to the dashboard summary — backend/app/utils/dashboard_metrics.py's `calculate_dashboard_summary()` (wired at backend/app/routers/test_runs.py line 211, endpoint `get_dashboard_summary` line 188) already builds a `durations` list (line 31) but only returns `avg_duration_s`. backend/app/utils/percentile.py's `percentile(values, p)` — docstring literally says "for run-duration widgets" — is a fully-tested pure function with zero callers anywhere in backend/app (verified via grep). Add `p50_duration_s`/`p95_duration_s` fields to calculate_dashboard_summary's return dict using `percentile(durations, 50)`/`percentile(durations, 95)` (guard the empty-runs early-return path with 0.0 defaults), and add the two fields to the `get_dashboard_summary` response dict in test_runs.py. {cat:backend; size:S; multifile:yes; research:none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Consolidate duplicate duration-bucket helpers and add a duration-distribution histogram to the dashboard — backend/app/utils/duration_bucket.py's `bucket(seconds)` (fast <5s / ok <30s / slow <120s / very_slow) and backend/app/utils/elapsed_bucket.py's `elapsed_bucket(seconds)` (<1s / 1-5s / 5-30s / 30s-2m / >2m) are two independent, zero-caller implementations of the same seconds-to-label bucketing concept (verified via grep — each referenced only by its own test file). Delete elapsed_bucket.py and its test (backend/tests/test_elapsed_bucket.py), then wire the survivor into backend/app/utils/dashboard_metrics.py's `calculate_dashboard_summary()`: bucket every value in the existing `durations` list (line 31) with `duration_bucket.bucket()` and add a `duration_distribution` dict (e.g. `{"fast": 3, "ok": 5, "slow": 1, "very_slow": 0}`) to the returned summary, surfaced in the `get_dashboard_summary` response (backend/app/routers/test_runs.py line 188). {cat:backend; size:M; multifile:yes; research:none}

## P2/P3 — round 5 audit (2026-09-17: fleet ran dry again — 0 ready, 0 backlog on this repo. Confirmed the earlier 2026-09-17 fixes (0-byte test debris in backend/tests/, dead root-level tests/+pytest.ini scaffold) are in and did not re-find those two issues. Went beyond the zero-caller-util pattern already mined heavily by rounds 1-4: traced the actual upload/confirm flow end-to-end, checked frontend Pinia store test coverage directly, and found two more zero-caller utils rounds 1-4's audits missed plus one dead orphaned Vue file.)
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Fix the non-functional upload confirm/status/list/delete flow — `POST /api/uploads/test-plan/{upload_id}/confirm` (backend/app/routers/uploads.py, `confirm_upload`) unconditionally raises `404 "Upload not found"` for every upload_id (comment: "For demo, we don't track uploads, so return 404"); `GET /api/uploads/test-plan/{upload_id}/status` always returns hardcoded placeholder data (`status: "processed"`, `test_count: 5`, `app_url: "https://example.com"`) regardless of the real upload_id passed in; `GET /api/uploads` always returns `[]`; `DELETE /api/uploads/{upload_id}` always reports success without persisting anything. Root cause: no Upload model exists at all (verified: `grep -rn 'class Upload' backend/app/models/` -> no match). ## Newly discovered (2026-09-18 refuel round 3 — full roadmap exhaustion)
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Remove the live-request-path call to process_dead_code_removal() from the orchestrator's YAML parser — backend/app/agents/orchestrator.py line 150 calls `process_dead_code_removal()` (imported at line 10 from backend/app/services/self_healing.py line 117) whenever a customer-uploaded YAML test plan parses to a non-dict (e.g. a plain YAML list) — a common, benign user mistake reachable through the real upload/parse flow (`TestOrchestrator.parse_test_plan`). That function walks the ENTIRE repo tree via `os.walk(repo_root=".")`, and for every `.py` file strips any `import `/`from ` line whenever the module name isn't found as a raw substring anywhere else in the file (a naive check that false-positives constantly — e.g. a module named `os` matches the substring "os" inside dozens of unrelated words like "cross"/"most"/"post"), silently overwriting files in place, then runs `git ls-files` + `git rm --cached` on every zero-byte tracked file repo-wide. Verified live and unmocked: backend/tests/test_orchestrator_parsing.py::test_parse_non_dict_yaml_returns_invalid (line 378, `raw_yaml = "- item1\n- item2\n"`) exercises this exact branch with no monkeypatching of process_dead_code_removal, so every real test run mutates the actual working tree — this exactly matches the ~300-file spurious "unused import" diff observed in a fresh local checkout of this repo before this fix (dead giveaway: `from contextlib import asynccontextmanager` silently stripped from backend/app/main.py). Delete the call site (orchestrator.py lines 10 and 150) so a malformed upload just returns the validation error, and delete `process_dead_code_removal()` from self_healing.py (lines 117-203) entirely since it has no legitimate caller and must never be reachable from a live request path regardless of the original quarantine-file-cleanup intent it was built for. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Fix the SSRF hole in webhook URL validation — backend/app/routers/webhooks.py's `validate_url()` (line 38) explicitly accepts `localhost`, any private/loopback IPv4 address, and even `0.0.0.0` as a valid webhook target. Verified by the repo's own test suite: backend/tests/test_webhooks_validate_url.py asserts `validate_url("http://localhost:8000")` (line 16), `validate_url("http://192.168.1.1")` (line 19), `validate_url("http://10.0.0.1:8080")` (line 22), and `validate_url("http://0.0.0.0")` (line 61) are all `True`. `POST /api/webhooks` (webhooks.py ~line 99-123) uses this as its sole gate before persisting a `WebhookEndpoint.url`, and backend/app/services/notification_dispatcher.py's `_send_request` later makes a real outbound HTTP request to that stored URL on every matching event with no further check — so any authenticated customer can register a webhook pointing at an internal-only host (loopback, RFC1918 ranges, or a cloud metadata endpoint like 169.254.169.254) and have the server issue requests to it on their behalf. Add a private/loopback/link-local host check (Python's `ipaddress` module, resolving the hostname first if it isn't already a literal IP) to `validate_url()` and reject those hosts; update the now-incorrect acceptance assertions in test_webhooks_validate_url.py to expect rejection instead. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Wire mask_email() into the three log lines that print raw customer email addresses — backend/app/utils/mask_email.py's `mask_email(addr)` (fully tested, e.g. `john.doe@example.com` -> `j***@example.com`) has zero callers anywhere in backend/app (verified: `grep -rn 'mask_email' backend/app --include='*.py'` matches only the file itself and its own test). Three call sites still log the full plaintext address: backend/app/routers/auth.py line 104 (`logger.info(f"New user {user.email} signed up; created org {org.slug}")`), backend/app/services/email_service.py line 80 (`logger.info("SMTP not configured; skipping email to %s for %s", to_address, event)`), and line 93 (`logger.warning("Email send to %s failed: %s", to_address, exc)`). Replace each raw `user.email`/`to_address` interpolation with `mask_email(...)` so customer PII doesn't land in plaintext application logs. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Add jitter to webhook delivery retry backoff — backend/app/utils/backoff_jitter.py's `jittered(delay_ms, jitter_ratio, rand=random.random)` (fully tested pure helper) has zero callers anywhere in backend/app (verified via grep — only its own test references it). backend/app/services/notification_dispatcher.py's `_attempt_delivery()` (line 97) builds its retry delay schedule via `retry_schedule()` (backend/app/utils/retry_backoff.py) with no jitter, so when many webhook deliveries fail at once (e.g. a customer endpoint has a brief outage), every pending retry for every affected delivery wakes up and re-fires at exactly the same capped-exponential intervals — a thundering-herd pattern against the customer's own endpoint. Wrap each computed delay in `jittered(delay_ms, jitter_ratio=0.2)` before the `await asyncio.sleep(delay_ms / 1000)` call at line 109. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Refuel 2026-09-19 — roadmap ran dry again (0 [ready], everything [decomposed]; backlog/doable still had runway at refuel time, so this is a get-ahead pass, not a stuck-repo recovery). 4 new gaps verified by direct code read.
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] `is_safe_url()`'s blocking DNS resolution runs synchronously inside async request/delivery handlers, on the hot path of every webhook send — `backend/app/utils/ssrf_guard.py`'s `is_safe_url()` calls `socket.getaddrinfo()` (line ~25), a blocking syscall with no timeout, directly (no `await`, no `asyncio.to_thread`/executor offload). It is called from two places: `backend/app/routers/webhooks.py`'s `register_webhook` (an `async def` route, line 102: `elif not validate_url(url):` -> `validate_url()` -> `is_safe_url()`), and — worse — `backend/app/services/notification_dispatcher.py`'s `_send_request()` (line 49, `if not is_safe_url(url):`), which is the actual network-send function invoked on **every webhook delivery attempt and every retry** for every customer's every subscribed event (confirmed via `grep -n 'is_safe_url' backend/app/services/notification_dispatcher.py backend/app/routers/webhooks.py`). A slow-to-resolve or non-responding hostname (attacker-controlled or just a flaky DNS server) blocks this app's single-threaded asyncio event loop for the full DNS timeout on every single delivery, stalling every other concurrent request/response in the process — an availability bug hiding inside the code that's supposed to be a security hardening measure. Wrap the `is_safe_url()` call in `await asyncio.to_thread(is_safe_url, url)` at both call sites (or switch to an async-native resolver), and add a regression test that a slow/blocking fake resolver doesn't stall a concurrent request during delivery. {cat:backend; size:S; multifile:yes; research:none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Dead duplicate scheduling-due-check in `utils/schedule.py`, superseded by `cron_match.py` — `backend/app/utils/schedule.py`'s `next_run_after(interval_minutes, from_dt)` and `is_due(scheduled_dt, now)` (both fully tested in `backend/tests/test_schedule.py`) are confirmed via `grep -rn 'is_due(|next_run_after(|utils.schedule' backend --include='*.py'` to have zero callers anywhere outside their own test file. The real, wired scheduling logic uses a completely different module instead: `backend/app/utils/cron_match.py`'s `cron_matches()`, called from `backend/app/routers/ci.py` (lines 301/317) and `backend/app/services/scheduling.py` (line 82, inside `run_due_schedules()` which is itself wired via `backend/app/services/schedule_dispatcher.py` and `POST /ci/schedules/run-due`). `schedule.py`'s interval-based due-check was apparently an earlier, since-abandoned approach to the same "is this scheduled thing due" problem the cron-based system now solves for real. - [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Two aider-authored files landed inside `backend/app/utils/` instead of `backend/tests/`, so they silently never run and hide two dead orphaned utilities — `backend/app/utils/test_pct.py` (a `TestPct` class with 9 real assertions against `app.utils.pct.pct`) and `backend/app/utils/test_test_runs_query.py` (4 real `test_*` functions against `app.utils.test_runs_query.buildTestRunsQueryParams`) are genuine pytest test files, but `backend/pytest.ini` scopes collection to `testpaths = tests` (confirmed: `grep -n testpaths backend/pytest.ini`), so pytest never discovers or runs either one — confirmed via `pytest --cov=app --cov-report=term-missing`, which shows both files at **0% covered** as plain (uncollected) app source, not as executed tests. This is a variant of this portfolio's known "misplaced/empty test file gives false confidence" bug class (see the shared memory note on the 2026-09-17 empty-test-file false-red), except inverted here: instead of a red gate, it's a SILENT gap — a green suite that looks like it covers `pct()`/`buildTestRunsQueryParams()` but never actually runs those assertions at all. Fix: `git mv backend/app/utils/test_pct.py backend/tests/test_pct.py` and `git mv backend/app/utils/test_test_runs_query.py backend/tests/test_test_runs_query.py` so they actually run, then decide `pct()`/`buildTestRunsQueryParams()`'s fate on their merits — `pct()` duplicates the already-flagged, similarly-dead `frontend/src/utils/pct.ts` orphan (see this roadmap's earlier item) and a backend caller may not be needed; `buildTestRunsQueryParams()` should either get a real backend caller (e.g. `backend/app/routers/test_runs.py`'s list endpoint, which currently builds its own query params inline) or be deleted alongside its now-relocated test. ## Refuel 2026-09-25 (Claude research pass — roadmap was fully [decomposed]/dry)

Verified live against the real repo (not just the scratchpad snapshot). Rounds 1-5 had already mined nearly every zero-caller-util/dead-code angle in `backend/app/utils`, so this pass instead traced real end-to-end flows (CI scheduling, usage/billing, upload, reporting) and found several functional gaps the earlier util-level audits couldn't have caught.

- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Add a test-plan field to the CI Integrations dashboard forms so GitHub-triggered and cron-scheduled runs actually run tests — traced the CI-integrations dashboard end-to-end and found the feature is silently broken for any customer who only uses the UI (not the raw API): `frontend/src/pages/CiIntegrationsPage.vue`'s "Add Integration" form (line 15) only collects `repo_full_name`/`events` (`newIntegration = reactive({ repo_full_name: '', events: ['push'] })`, line 143) and its "Add Schedule" form (line 84) only collects `name`/`cron` (`newSchedule = reactive({ name: '', cron: '' })`, line 144) — neither ever sends a `test_plan`/`default_test_plan` field, and both are passed straight through to `store.createIntegration(payload)`/`store.createSchedule(payload)` (`frontend/src/stores/ci.js` lines 26/62) with no default injected. Server-side, `backend/app/routers/ci.py`'s `IntegrationCreate.default_test_plan`/`ScheduleCreate.test_plan` (lines 36/51) both default to `{}` via `Field(default_factory=dict)`, and `backend/app/models/ci.py`'s `GitHubIntegration.default_test_plan`/`ScheduledRun.test_plan` (lines 21/37) persist that empty dict as-is. `backend/app/services/scheduling.py`'s `create_run()` (line 27) computes `total_tests=len(test_plan.get("tests", []) or [])`, so every CI-triggered and cron-scheduled run created through the dashboard executes a 0-test plan. Add a YAML/JSON textarea (reuse the parse-and-validate pattern already in `frontend/src/pages/TestPlanEditorPage.vue`) to `frontend/src/pages/CiIntegrationsPage.vue`'s "Add Integration" form (wire to `IntegrationCreate.default_test_plan`) and "Add Schedule" form (wire to `ScheduleCreate.test_plan`), and update `frontend/src/stores/ci.js`'s `createIntegration`/`createSchedule` payloads accordingly; add a frontend test asserting a non-empty `test_plan`/`default_test_plan` is included in the POST body. {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}

- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Replace fabricated duration/cost estimates in `GET /api/usage/metrics` with real data already on hand — the Usage page is customer-facing on a billed SaaS product, so this was checked for accuracy: `backend/app/routers/usage.py`'s `get_usage_metrics` (lines ~44-61) computes `total_duration_minutes = (total_tests * 10) / 60` (a hardcoded 10-seconds-per-test guess) and `estimated_cost = ai_calls * 0.0005` (`ai_calls = total_tests * 2.5`, also hardcoded) despite the same handler already holding the full `test_runs` list with each row's real `duration_ms` column (`backend/app/models/test_run.py` line 62) in scope — the endpoint ignores real data it already fetched. The identical magic-constant formulas are duplicated (not reused, independently re-typed) in `backend/app/utils/usage_metrics.py`'s `total_duration_minutes()`/`estimated_ai_calls()`/`estimated_cost()`, confirming this was never wired to anything real. Replace `total_duration_minutes` with a sum of `run.duration_ms` across the queried `test_runs` (falling back to the existing estimate only for runs with a null `duration_ms`). {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] **[HUMAN/design]** Track real per-run LLM token usage so `/api/usage/metrics` cost figures are real, not guessed — once duration is fixed (item above), cost/AI-call accuracy is the harder half of the same problem. `backend/app/models/test_run.py` has no column for input/output tokens (only a string `llm_tier`, line 32), and `backend/app/services/llm_service.py`'s `estimate_cost(model, input_tokens, output_tokens)` (line 88, real per-model-rate calculator, unit-tested in `backend/tests/test_llm_service_estimate_cost.py`) has zero callers anywhere in `backend/app` (verified via grep) — a correct cost calculator exists but nothing feeds it real token counts from the analyzer/executor/optimizer agents' actual Claude API calls. Needs a design call (per-call usage rows vs. a running total column on `TestRun`, and whether to backfill or only apply going forward) before decomposing into backend tasks. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}

- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Expose browser selection on the actual upload flow, not just the separate YAML-editor tool — the product roadmap explicitly lists cross-browser testing as a target feature; checked whether it's actually reachable by a real customer today and found the backend fully supports it but the primary UI path hides it entirely. `frontend/src/pages/UploadPage.vue` (the real per-README "upload a test plan" flow most customers use) has zero `browser_type` control anywhere (verified: `grep -n browser_type frontend/src/pages/UploadPage.vue` returns nothing); only the separate `frontend/src/pages/TestPlanEditorPage.vue` (a YAML validate/dry-run tool) has a browser dropdown (`browserType` ref, line 148) that injects a `browser_type: "..."` line into pasted YAML text. Meanwhile the backend fully supports firefox/webkit end-to-end already: `backend/app/services/execution_engine.py` line 601 reads `test_plan.get("browser_type")`, `backend/app/services/playwright_service.py`'s `PlaywrightService.__init__` (line 32) validates against `SUPPORTED_BROWSERS`, and `backend/app/services/test_plan_validation.py` line 211 already returns `valid_browser_types` from the validate endpoint. Add the same browser-select control (chromium/firefox/webkit) to `UploadPage.vue` and have it write `browser_type` into the parsed plan before upload, so cross-browser support (PRODUCT_ROADMAP.md Q4 Tier 3) is actually reachable from the main product surface. {cat: web; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] **[HUMAN/design]** CI schedules/integrations each embed an independent raw `test_plan` copy with no shared source of truth — `backend/app/models/ci.py`'s `GitHubIntegration.default_test_plan` (line 21) and `ScheduledRun.test_plan` (line 37) are both plain JSON blobs with no reference to an `Upload` row; `backend/app/routers/ci.py`'s create endpoints (lines 93, 257, 326) just persist whatever raw dict is passed. Once the item above gives customers a UI path to attach a plan, a customer who fixes a bug in their YAML still has to re-paste the corrected plan into every integration/schedule that uses it separately, with no single place to update it once. Needs a real design call — reference `Upload.id` (single source of truth, but a deleted/edited upload could silently change a schedule's behavior) vs. keep embedded snapshots (current behavior, arguably intentional for CI reproducibility) — before this becomes a decomposable backlog item. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}

- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Add duration-regression detection per test, mirroring the already-shipped visual-regression pattern — this repo already computes and surfaces "did this test's screenshot regress vs. the previous run" (`backend/app/utils/visual_diff.py`, wired into `backend/app/services/execution_engine.py`) and "is this test's flake trend improving/worsening" (`backend/app/utils/flake_trend.py`'s `trend_direction`, wired at `GET /api/flakes/{test_name}/trend`), but nothing does the equivalent for a test's `duration_ms` — a test that silently goes from 2s to 20s per run raises no flag anywhere in the dashboard today (verified: no `duration_regression`/`perf_regression`/`duration_trend` symbol exists anywhere in `backend/app`). Add a `duration_regression` flag using the same "look up the most recent prior run(s) of the same `test_plan_name`+`test_name`" lookup already implemented for visual-regression, comparing current `duration_ms` against a trailing average/threshold, and surface it next to the existing flake/visual-regression badges on `frontend/src/pages/TestRunDetailPage.vue`. {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}

- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Add an HTML export option to the test-run report, closing the gap against PRODUCT_ROADMAP.md's stated "Download results (JSON/HTML report)" beta feature — `GET /api/test-runs/{run_id}/report` (`backend/app/routers/test_runs.py`, `get_test_run_report`, line 300) only ever returns JSON; there is no HTML variant, template, or export anywhere in the repo (verified: no `templates/` dir, no `jinja2` import anywhere in `backend/app`). Add a `?format=html` query option (or a sibling `/report.html` route) that renders the same summary + per-test timeline + failure-diagnosis data already assembled by the existing handler into a single static HTML page, so a customer can share/archive a run result without needing dashboard access. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Auto-research 2026-10-18 (ovn_auto_research.sh; 7 item(s))
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] `start_test_run` allows re-executing terminal runs — `backend/app/routers/test_runs.py:216-235` only guards against RUNNING (returns 200) and INITIALIZING (raises 400); no check for COMPLETED, FAILED, CANCELLED, or FLAKY. Calling `POST /{run_id}/start` on a completed run sets `status=RUNNING`, nulls `completed_at`, and fires a new background task that overwrites all result data. Fix: add a check before line 228 raising HTTP 409 when `test_run.status in (TestRunStatus.COMPLETED, TestRunStatus.FAILED, TestRunStatus.CANCELLED, TestRunStatus.FLAKY)`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] HTML report format=html silently returns empty content — `backend/app/routers/test_runs.py:417-435` builds `report_data` with keys `"run"`, `"timeline"`, `"artifact_count"` etc., but `backend/app/utils/html_report.py:20-22` reads `"summary"`, `"tests"`, `"diagnosis"` — none of which appear in the caller's dict, so every rendered report shows Total:0 with no test rows. Second bug: the handler `return render_html_report(report_data)` (line 435) returns a plain string that FastAPI JSON-encodes; it must return `Response(content=html, media_type="text/html")`. The `format=html` path has no integration test. Fix both: align the dict keys and wrap the return. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] `cancel_test_run` overwrites terminal-status runs — `backend/app/routers/test_runs.py:539-568` unconditionally sets `status=CANCELLED` and updates `completed_at` on any run owned by the user, including ones already COMPLETED or FAILED. A user who clicks Cancel after a run finishes silently mutates a good result to CANCELLED. Fix: add a terminal-status guard before line 555 raising HTTP 409 when `test_run.status in (TestRunStatus.COMPLETED, TestRunStatus.FAILED, TestRunStatus.CANCELLED)`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] `TERMINAL_STATUSES` and `VALID_STATUSES` missing `"flaky"` — `backend/app/utils/run_status.py:8-23` defines `VALID_STATUSES` and `TERMINAL_STATUSES` without `"flaky"`, even though `TestRunStatus.FLAKY = "flaky"` was added to `backend/app/models/test_run.py:18` and the DB via migration `0007`. `is_terminal("flaky")` returns `False`, meaning any caller using `is_terminal` to gate terminal-state logic would incorrectly treat a FLAKY run as still-active. `can_transition("running", "flaky")` also returns `False`. Fix: add `"flaky"` to both sets in `run_status.py`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] `run.recovered` webhook event not subscribable — `backend/app/services/recovery.py:77` dispatches `"run.recovered"` for stale runs, but `backend/app/routers/webhooks.py:47-56`'s `validate_event()` `valid_events` list does not include `"run.recovered"`. Users who try to register a webhook for that exact event string are rejected; only the `"run.*"` wildcard captures it. Fix: add `"run.recovered"` to `valid_events` in `validate_event()`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] FLAKY run status is unreachable: call site never passes flaky count — `backend/app/services/execution_engine.py:810` calls `derive_run_status(passed, failed)` with only two args, so `flaky` defaults to 0 and the `"flaky"` return branch in `backend/app/utils/run_status.py:84-85` is never reached. Runs with healed-but-passed tests (tests where `result.get("healing_events")` is non-empty and `status=="passed"`) should be counted as flaky. Fix: in `_drive` (line 614), track a `flaky` counter for tests where `status=="passed"` and `len(result.get("healing_events") or []) > 0`; decrement `passed` for those; pass `derive_run_status(passed, failed, flaky)` at line 810 and propagate the count through `_finalize`. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `pause_test_run` and `resume_test_run` lack terminal-state guards — `backend/app/routers/test_runs.py:571-618`: `pause_test_run` (line 587) sets `status=PAUSED` on any run including COMPLETED or FAILED; `resume_test_run` (line 612) sets `status=RUNNING` without verifying the run is currently PAUSED. Both can corrupt final-state data. Fix: in `pause_test_run`, raise HTTP 409 if `test_run.status not in (TestRunStatus.RUNNING,)`; in `resume_test_run`, raise HTTP 409 if `test_run.status != TestRunStatus.PAUSED`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Auto-research 2026-10-20 (ovn_auto_research.sh; 3 item(s))
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] `rerun_failed_tests` bypasses monthly run quota — `backend/app/routers/test_runs.py:rerun_failed_tests` (line 648) calls `billing.record_usage` (line 729) but never calls `billing.enforce_quota`, so a customer who has exhausted their tier's monthly run quota can silently create unlimited new test runs via `POST /{run_id}/rerun-failed`. The correct pattern (live-tier lookup + enforce_quota + graceful fallback) is right there at lines 115-129 in `create_test_run` in the same file. Fix: add `get_live_subscription_tier`/`enforce_quota` call with the same try/except structure before `db.add(new_test_run)` at line 724. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] HTML report always shows "Started: N/A" — `backend/app/utils/html_report.py:27` reads `run.get('started_at', 'N/A')`, but `backend/app/routers/test_runs.py`'s HTML branch (lines 425-441) builds `report_data["run"]` with only `'created_at'` and `'completed_at'` — no `'started_at'` key. Every `GET /api/test-runs/{run_id}/report?format=html` response silently shows "Started: N/A". `backend/tests/test_html_report.py:21` masks this by explicitly passing `"started_at"` in the test fixture. Fix: add `"started_at": run.created_at.isoformat() if run.created_at else None` to `report_data["run"]` in `test_runs.py`, and update `test_html_report.py` to verify the value isn't N/A. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `TestRun.flaky_tests` column missing — flaky count discarded after every run — `backend/app/models/test_run.py` has `passed_tests`/`failed_tests`/`skipped_tests` but no `flaky_tests` column. `backend/app/services/execution_engine.py` correctly computes `flaky_count` at lines 821/850 and passes it to `derive_run_status()` to set `TestRunStatus.FLAKY`, but `_finalize` (line 594) has no `flaky` parameter and never persists the count. As a result `run.passed_tests` overcounts healed tests, and `backend/app/routers/test_runs.py:488` uses `calculate_flake_ratio(failed, total_executed)` — failure rate, not flake rate — as a workaround. Fix: add `flaky_tests = Column(Integer, default=0)` to `TestRun`, add an Alembic migration, add `flaky: int = 0` to `_finalize` and set `run.flaky_tests = flaky`, pass `flaky_count` from the two `_finalize` call sites, and update the report endpoint to use `run.flaky_tests`. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}

## Auto-research 2026-10-20 (ovn_auto_research.sh; 4 item(s))
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `POST /{run_id}/start` silently re-executes PAUSED runs from scratch, discarding all progress — `backend/app/routers/test_runs.py` lines 218–243: the guard chain handles RUNNING (early return, line 218), TERMINAL (409, line 223), and INITIALIZING (400, line 229), but PAUSED falls through to `test_run.status = TestRunStatus.RUNNING` + `schedule_execution(test_run)` at lines 238–243, launching a brand-new background task that overwrites all prior phase results. The correct API is `POST /resume`; `/start` on a PAUSED run should return HTTP 409. Fix: add `if test_run.status == TestRunStatus.PAUSED: raise HTTPException(409, "Run is paused; use /resume to unpause")` before line 238. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none} {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `duration_regression` is always NULL for FLAKY runs — `backend/app/services/execution_engine.py:603` guards `_compute_duration_regression()` with `if status == TestRunStatus.COMPLETED:`, so a FLAKY run (healed tests, non-zero `flaky_count`) never gets a regression check even when it ran 3× longer than baseline. `TestRun.duration_regression` stays NULL for every FLAKY run, silently hiding the "Duration Regression" badge on `frontend/src/pages/TestRunDetailPage.vue` for the status that most often signals flakiness-induced slowdowns. Fix: extend the guard to `if status in (TestRunStatus.COMPLETED, TestRunStatus.FLAKY):` so `_compute_duration_regression` also fires for FLAKY; the helper already queries only prior COMPLETED baselines, so it will still compare against clean runs. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Delete dead `backend/app/utils/recovery.py` (`find_stale_run_ids`) — zero callers anywhere in `backend/app` (verified: grep `find_stale_run_ids\|utils\.recovery` in backend/app returns only the definition; only `backend/tests/test_recovery_utils.py` references it). The real, wired recovery logic is `backend/app/services/recovery.py`'s `recover_stale_runs()`, imported and called from `backend/app/main.py` lifespan. The two implementations disagree on the stale threshold (5 min vs. 30 min in services/recovery.py) — the same behavioral-divergence pattern flagged for mask/schedule/event_pattern helpers earlier on this roadmap. Delete `backend/app/utils/recovery.py` and `backend/tests/test_recovery_utils.py`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Delete batch of post-round-5 zero-caller utils added since the 2026-09-22 audit — confirmed via grep that each has zero non-test callers in `backend/app`: `plan_snapshot.py` (`create_plan_snapshot`, only `backend/tests/test_plan_snapshot.py`), `plan_ref.py` (`parse_plan_ref`, only `backend/tests/test_plan_ref.py`), `strip_ansi.py` (`strip_ansi`, only `backend/tests/test_strip_ansi.py`), `truncate.py` (`truncate_middle`, only `backend/tests/test_utils_truncate.py`), `token_aggregator.py` (`aggregate_usage`, only `backend/tests/test_token_aggregator.py`) + `token_usage.py` (`TokenUsage`, imported only by dead `token_aggregator.py`); `token_usage.TokenUsage` additionally duplicates `backend/app/services/llm_service.py`'s own local `TokenUsage` class (line 12) which IS wired in production, so the util-layer variant is an incompatible orphan. Delete all six files and their four test files. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}

## Qwen explore 2026-10-22 09:12 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens performance; 1 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot 62b7785d01, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Fix O(N) cron scan in _calculate_next_run — `backend/app/routers/ci.py:396` (`_calculate_next_run`) The function scans up to 527,040 minutes (366 days) one minute at a time to find the next cron match. For sparse cron expressions (e.g., monthly on the 1st), this can take ~500k iterations, which is slow and unnecessary. Evidence: `candidate = (from_dt + timedelta(minutes=1)).replace(second=0, microsecond=0) for _ in range(366 * 24 * 60): if cron_matches(cron, candidate): return`. Fix: Use a smarter algorithm that jumps to the next valid day/hour/minute based on the cron fields, or use a library like croniter. Add backend/tests/test_cron_next_run_perf.py to verify correctness and performance. VERIFY: `cd backend && python3 -m pytest tests/test_cron_next_run_perf.py -q`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: no; research: none}

## Qwen explore 2026-10-22 11:06 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens a11y-ux; 3 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot 3d9630b791, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Add type="button" to BillingPage upgrade buttons — `frontend/src/pages/BillingPage.vue:33` (`template`) The 'Upgrade to Pro' and 'Upgrade to Enterprise' buttons are missing the 'type' attribute. They default to 'submit', which is incorrect for action buttons that do not submit a form. This violates the repo convention and can cause unexpected behavior if these buttons are ever placed inside a form element. Evidence: `<button @click="handleUpgrade('pro')" :disabled="checkoutLoading"`. Fix: Add 'type="button"' to both upgrade buttons in 'frontend/src/pages/BillingPage.vue'. Add a new test file 'frontend/tests/billing-a11y.test.ts' that mounts the component and asserts all non-submit buttons have 'type="button"'. Add src/tests/billing-a11y.test.ts. VERIFY: `cd frontend && npx vitest run src/tests/billing-a11y.test.ts`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: web; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Add aria-label to BrowserSelector select element — `frontend/src/components/BrowserSelector.vue:2` (`template`) The '<select>' element has no accessible name (no 'aria-label', no associated '<label>', and no visible text). Screen readers will announce it as a generic 'combobox' or 'listbox' without context, making it unusable for assistive technology users. Evidence: `<select :value="modelValue" @change="$emit('update:modelValue', $event.target.value)"`. Fix: Add 'aria-label="Select browser type"' to the '<select>' element in 'frontend/src/components/BrowserSelector.vue'. Add a new test file 'frontend/tests/browser-selector-a11y.test.ts' that mounts the component and asserts the select has an accessible name. Add src/tests/browser-selector-a11y.test.ts. VERIFY: `cd frontend && npx vitest run src/tests/browser-selector-a11y.test.ts`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: web; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Add role="alert" to CiIntegrationsPage error div — `frontend/src/pages/CiIntegrationsPage.vue:5` (`template`) The error message container lacks 'role="alert"' or 'aria-live="assertive"'. Screen readers will not announce the error when it appears dynamically, leaving users unaware of failed actions (e.g., failed integration creation). Evidence: `<div v-if="store.error" class="bg-red-50 text-red-700 p-4 rounded-lg mb-6"> {{ store.error }} </div>`. Fix: Add 'role="alert"' to the error div in 'frontend/src/pages/CiIntegrationsPage.vue'. Add a new test file 'frontend/tests/ci-integrations-error-a11y.test.ts' that mounts the component with an error state and asserts the error container has 'role="alert"'. Add src/tests/ci-integrations-error-a11y.test.ts. VERIFY: `cd frontend && npx vitest run src/tests/ci-integrations-error-a11y.test.ts`. ## Qwen explore 2026-10-24 15:12 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens performance; 2 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot 8dca36b387, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Use SQL aggregate for monthly usage instead of loading all runs — `backend/app/routers/usage.py:48` (`get_tier_limits`) The endpoint loads every TestRun object for the current month into Python memory to sum total_tests, causing unbounded memory usage and slow responses as monthly run count grows. Evidence: `result = await db.execute(query) month_runs = result.scalars().all() tests_used_this_month = sum(run.total_tests for run in month_runs)`. Fix: Replace the full-table load with a single SQL query using func.sum(TestRun.total_tests). Add backend/tests/test_tier_limits_aggregate.py to verify the endpoint returns correct usage without loading all rows. VERIFY: `cd backend && python3 -m pytest tests/test_tier_limits_aggregate.py -q`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Remove duplicate query execution in get_failure_patterns — `backend/app/services/knowledge_base.py:72` (`get_failure_patterns`) The function executes the same database query twice, doubling the database load and latency for every call. Evidence: `result = await self.db.execute(query) entries = result.scalars().all() result = await self.db.execute(query) entries = result.scalars().all()`. Fix: Remove the duplicate query execution block. Add backend/tests/test_knowledge_base_no_duplicate_query.py to verify the query is executed only once. VERIFY: `cd backend && python3 -m pytest tests/test_knowledge_base_no_duplicate_query.py -q`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Qwen explore 2026-10-27 17:05 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens type-safety; 1 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot e083cb0e72, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Fix division by zero in SQL order_by for knowledge base queries — `backend/app/services/knowledge_base.py:67` (`get_selector_patterns`) The SQL expression 'KnowledgeEntry.successes / KnowledgeEntry.attempts' will raise a division by zero error if any 'KnowledgeEntry' row has 'attempts = 0'. This causes the entire query to fail, preventing retrieval of selector patterns. Evidence: `).order_by( # Order by success rate (descending) (KnowledgeEntry.successes / KnowledgeEntry.attempts).desc() )`. Fix: Use a safe division expression in the order_by clause that handles zero attempts. For example: '(KnowledgeEntry.successes / func.coalesce(KnowledgeEntry.attempts, 1)).desc()' or use 'case' to handle zero attempts explicitly. Add tests/test_knowledge_base_division_safety.py. ## Seeded 2026-10-28 from ROADMAP_2026-11 (Mark-authorized)
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Python SDK typed errors and retries with mocked-transport tests (C3) — `sdk/python/specpilot_sdk/client.py` (61 lines) has only a generic `SpecPilotError` and no retry; its only test, `sdk/python/tests/test_client_live.py`, needs a live server. Add `SpecPilotAuthError` (401/403), `SpecPilotNotFoundError` (404), `SpecPilotRateLimitError` (429, carrying `retry_after` from the Retry-After header) and `SpecPilotServerError` (5xx) as subclasses of `SpecPilotError`, chosen in `_request`; add a `max_retries` constructor arg (default 2) that retries 429/5xx and `httpx.TransportError` with capped exponential backoff for GET requests only (never retry POST /runs); export the new classes in `specpilot_sdk/__init__.py`; add `sdk/python/tests/test_client_mocked.py` using `httpx.MockTransport` injected via the existing `client=` parameter, covering each error class, retry-then-succeed, retry exhaustion, and POST not retried. {cat: test; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Public API flake listing plus SDK `list_flakes` (C3/C5) — `backend/app/routers/public_api.py` exposes only runs and plan validation (`@router.get("/runs")`, `@router.post("/runs")`, `@router.post("/plans/validate")`), so SDK users cannot read flake data that `backend/app/services/flake_detection.py` `list_stats(db, customer_id, quarantined_only)` already computes. Add `GET /api/v1/flakes` (query: `quarantined_only`, `limit` 1-100) following the same `require_scope` and `public_api_limit` pattern as `list_runs`, returning the same fields as `backend/app/routers/flakes.py` `_serialize`, then add `list_flakes(self, quarantined_only=False, limit=50)` to `sdk/python/specpilot_sdk/client.py`. Add endpoint tests next to `backend/tests/test_public_api.py` (tenant scoping, quarantined_only filter, limit bounds). {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Deduplicate GitHub webhook deliveries by `X-GitHub-Delivery` (C7) — `backend/app/routers/ci.py` `github_webhook` (line ~285) creates a new run for every POST, so GitHub's redelivery of the same delivery id (manual redeliver, timeout retry) starts duplicate runs. Add a small bounded TTL set in a new `backend/app/utils/delivery_dedupe.py` (`seen(delivery_id) -> bool` that records the id, max 5000 entries, 24h TTL, injectable clock for tests), call it in `github_webhook` after signature verification and before `scheduling.create_run`, and return `{"status": "duplicate", "delivery_id": ...}` with 200 for a repeat; requests with no header are never treated as duplicates. Tests: `backend/tests/test_delivery_dedupe.py` for the util and a webhook test beside `backend/tests/test_ci_webhook_trigger.py`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Retry transient failures when posting GitHub commit statuses (C7) — `backend/app/services/github_ci.py` `_github_request` (line ~119) makes a single attempt, so a 502 or network blip silently loses the status check on the PR. Add up to 2 retries with short capped backoff (injectable sleep for tests) on `httpx.TransportError` and 5xx responses only (never on 4xx), keeping the existing result dict shape from `post_commit_status`/`update_check_run`. Tests beside `backend/tests/test_github_ci_update_check_run.py` using a fake client: 5xx-then-200 succeeds, persistent 5xx returns `ok: False` after 3 attempts, 422 is not retried. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Link the SpecPilot run from the GitHub commit status (C7) — `backend/app/routers/ci.py` `github_webhook` calls `github_ci.post_commit_status(repo_full_name, sha, run.status.value, description=...)` without `target_url`, although `post_commit_status` already accepts `target_url`, so the PR check has no link back to the run. Add a settings value (e.g. `public_frontend_url` in `backend/app/core/config.py`, default empty) and a pure helper `build_run_url(base_url, run_id) -> Optional[str]` (None when base_url is empty, strips trailing slash) in a new `backend/app/utils/run_url.py`; pass it as `target_url` in `github_webhook`. Tests: `backend/tests/test_run_url.py` plus a webhook test asserting `target_url` is forwarded when configured and omitted when not. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Usage page limit warning at 80 percent (C10) — `frontend/src/pages/UsagePage.vue` only turns the bar red at `limits.percent_used >= 100` (line ~28) and shows no warning text, even though `GET /api/usage/limits` already returns `percent_used`, `max_tests_per_month` and `resets_at`. Add a pure util `frontend/src/utils/usageWarning.ts` with `usageWarning(percentUsed, max)` returning `null` (under 80 or unlimited), `'approaching'` (80 to under 100) or `'exceeded'` (100 or more), render an amber banner for approaching and a red one for exceeded in `UsagePage.vue` (include the reset date), and add `frontend/src/utils/__tests__/usageWarning.test.ts` covering the boundaries 79, 80, 99, 100, unlimited. {cat: web; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Approve a visual diff as the new baseline (C2) — `backend/app/routers/visual_diffs.py` has only `GET ""` and `GET "/{result_id}"`; `backend/app/utils/baseline_lookup.py` `get_baseline_artifact` always picks the most recent other-run screenshot, so a user cannot pin a reviewed screenshot as the baseline. ## Seeded round 7 2026-11-06 (Mark-authorized)
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `cron_matches` in backend/app/utils/cron_match.py only validates the fields it happens to evaluate: the final `return` chains `cron_field_matches(minute...) and cron_field_matches(hour...) and day_ok and cron_field_matches(month...)`, so once the minute field does not match "now" the hour and month fields are never parsed. `POST /api/ci/schedules` (create_schedule in backend/app/routers/ci.py) uses `cron_matches(body.cron, datetime.now(UTC))` as its ONLY syntax validator, so an invalid cron is accepted whenever the minute does not match the current minute, then never fires and shows next_run_at null. Verified with python3 -I on the exported source: cron_matches("59 25 * * *", datetime(2026,11,6,10,30)) returns False and cron_matches("0 0 1 13 *", same dt) returns False, while cron_matches("61 10 * * *", same dt) raises ValueError (minute is parsed first). Fix: in cron_matches parse all five fields up front (compute every cron_field_matches result into local variables, or call _parse_field on all five) BEFORE combining with and/or, keeping the existing dom/dow OR semantics and return values unchanged. Tests in new file backend/tests/test_cron_matches_validates_all_fields.py: parametrize invalid expressions "59 25 * * *", "0 0 1 13 *", "0 0 * 0 *", "0 24 * * *" at a dt whose minute does not match and assert pytest.raises(ValueError); also assert valid "30 10 * * *" still True at 10:30 and False at 10:31. - [ ] [P2] [decomposed] `verify_github_signature` in backend/app/services/github_ci.py raises TypeError for a non-ASCII `X-Hub-Signature-256` header instead of returning False: after the `sha256=` prefix check it calls `hmac.compare_digest(expected, signature_header)` with two str values, and compare_digest rejects non-ASCII str. The GitHub webhook route (POST /api/ci/github webhook in backend/app/routers/ci.py) is unauthenticated, so any caller can turn a bad signature into an unhandled 500 with a header like b"sha256=\xe9" (starlette decodes it as latin-1 'e-acute'). Verified by exec-ing the function source under python3 -I: verify_github_signature("s", b"b", "sha256=\u00e9") raises TypeError "comparing strings with non-ASCII characters is not supported". Fix: compare bytes, i.e. hmac.compare_digest(expected.encode("utf-8"), signature_header.encode("utf-8", "replace")), leaving all other branches (empty secret/header, missing prefix, valid signature) unchanged. Tests in new file backend/tests/test_github_signature_non_ascii.py: non-ASCII header returns False (not raises), "sha256=" plus 64 non-hex ASCII chars returns False, a correctly computed signature still returns True, and bytes body with unicode secret still verifies. VERIFY: `cd backend && python3 -m pytest tests/test_github_signature_non_ascii.py tests/test_ci_webhook_signature.py -q`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

Verified with python3 -I: select_tests_by_risk([{"name":"a","risk_score":None}], 0.5) raises TypeError (float() argument must be ... not NoneType) and risk_score "abc" / "high" raise ValueError; the docstring only promises a missing key counts as 0.0. Fix: wrap the conversion in a small helper that returns 0.0 for None, bool, non-numeric strings and NaN (try float(...) except (TypeError, ValueError)), keep numeric strings like "0.7" working, keep the >= threshold rule and output order. Tests in new file backend/tests/test_select_tests_by_risk_bad_scores.py: None, "abc", [] and float("nan") scores are treated as 0.0 (selected only when threshold <= 0), "0.9" string is selected at threshold 0.5, valid float scores unchanged, empty list returns []. - [ ] [P3] [decomposed] `backoff_delay_ms` in backend/app/utils/retry_backoff.py raises OverflowError for large attempt numbers and returns a NEGATIVE delay for a negative cap: it computes `base_ms * (multiplier ** (attempt - 1))` as a float before clamping to cap_ms, and its docstring promises the result is "clamped to [0, cap_ms]". Verified with python3 -I: backoff_delay_ms(2000, 100, 1000) raises OverflowError (34, 'Result too large'), and backoff_delay_ms(1, 100, -5) returns -5. It is wired in services/self_healing.py (max_retries) and via retry_schedule in services/notification_dispatcher.py (settings.webhook_max_retries, env-configurable), so a large configured retry count blows up delivery instead of capping. Fix: catch OverflowError around the exponent (treat as cap_ms) and clamp with max(0, min(delay, cap_ms)); keep int() truncation and the base_ms <= 0 -> 0 rule. Tests in new file backend/tests/test_backoff_delay_overflow.py: attempt 2000 returns cap_ms, retry_schedule(1100, 100, 1000)[-1] == 1000 without raising, negative cap returns 0, existing small cases unchanged (attempt 1..3 of base 100 give 100, 200, 400). - [ ] [P3] [decomposed] `redact_secrets` in backend/app/utils/redact.py both over-redacts and under-redacts: `_SK_RE = re.compile(r"sk_\S+")` has no word boundary so it matches the 'sk_' inside ordinary words, and `_BEARER_RE = re.compile(r"Bearer\s+\S+")` is case-sensitive so a lowercase scheme leaks the token. Verified with python3 -I: extract_host("http://[::1]/x") returns "[:" (should be "::1"), extract_host("http://user:pw@Host.com:80/p") returns "user:pw@host.com" (should be "host.com"), while "http://[::1]:8080/x" returns "[::1]" (brackets kept, inconsistent with the portless case). Fix: use urlparse(url).hostname (which lowercases and strips userinfo, brackets and port); for scheme-less input retry with urlparse("//" + url).hostname so "example.com/path" and "host:80" still work; keep None for empty input and for unparsable/no-host input; keep existing documented examples. Fix: replace the import with `try: import jwt` / `except ImportError: from jose import jwt` (keep the ValueError only if both imports fail) so the function works with the installed dependency; claims stay `iat`, `exp = iat + 600`, `iss = app_id`. VERIFY: `cd backend && python3 -m pytest tests/test_github_installation_token.py -q` and confirm passes. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Seeded round 18 2026-11-15 (Mark-authorized)
### FEATURE: Markdown run report - acceptance: `backend/app/utils/html_report.py` renders a run as HTML only, so a team that wants to paste a run into an issue, a pull request or a chat has to rebuild a table by hand. Add a pure Markdown renderer that takes the same `{'run': ..., 'timeline': [...], 'artifact_count': n}` dict as `render_html_report`. Backend utils only, no endpoint, no change to `html_report.py`.

## Seeded round 21 2026-11-18 (Mark-authorized)
### FEATURE: Run comparison endpoint and page route - acceptance: `GET /api/test-runs/compare?run_a_id=<id>&run_b_id=<id>` returns the summary and per-test table that `frontend/src/pages/RunComparePage.vue` already asks for (that page calls this endpoint today but no backend route exists and the page has no route), and the page is reachable at `/runs/compare` from a link on the runs page; every existing endpoint and the page body are unchanged.
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Add the comparison table builder - NEW `backend/app/utils/run_compare_table.py` with `compare_status_tables(results_a: list[dict], results_b: list[dict]) -> dict` returning `{"summary": {"total_tests": t, "passed": p, "failed": f}, "tests": [...]}` where `tests` holds one `{"name": n, "run_a_status": a, "run_b_status": b, "change": c}` per distinct `test_name` found in either list ordered by name (a name repeated inside one list keeps its last status), a status absent from one run is the string `missing`, `c` is `regressed` when `a == "passed"` and `b == "failed"`, `improved` when `a == "failed"` and `b == "passed"` and `unchanged` otherwise, and the summary counts the entries of `tests` (`t`), those whose `run_b_status` is `passed` (`p`) and `failed` (`f`). Create `backend/tests/utils/test_run_compare_table.py` with `test_union_of_names_sorted`, `test_missing_status`, `test_regressed_and_improved`, `test_unchanged_for_same_status` and `test_summary_counts_use_run_b`. VERIFY: `cd backend && python3 -m pytest tests/utils/test_run_compare_table.py -q`. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Add the compare router and mount it before the run routes - NEW `backend/app/routers/run_compare.py` with `router = APIRouter(prefix="/api/test-runs", tags=["test-runs"])` and `GET /compare` (`summary="Compare two test runs"`) taking `run_a_id: str = Query(...)`, `run_b_id: str = Query(...)`, `current_user: User = Depends(get_current_user)` (both from `app.core.auth`) and `db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_db)`: load both `TestRun` rows with `customer_id == current_user.customer_id` (answer 404 `Test run not found` when either is missing), load each run's `TestResult` rows ordered by `created_at, id` and return `{**compare_status_tables(list_a, list_b), "run_a": {"id": a.id, "status": a.status.value}, "run_b": {"id": b.id, "status": b.status.value}}` where each list holds `{"test_name": r.test_name, "status": r.status}`. ### FEATURE: Delete a finished test run - acceptance: an owner can delete a completed, failed, cancelled or flaky run together with its results and stored artifact files from the run detail page, so a local or Docker install can reclaim disk; a run that is still active is refused and no existing endpoint changes.

Create `backend/tests/test_run_deletion_service.py` with async tests using `test_session_factory` and `ArtifactStorage(root=str(tmp_path))` (seed rows like `_seed` in `backend/tests/test_artifacts_endpoints.py`): `test_deletes_rows_and_files`, `test_missing_file_does_not_block_deletion`, `test_active_run_is_kept`, `test_other_customers_run_is_not_found` and `test_unknown_run_is_not_found`. - [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Add the delete endpoint and mount it - NEW `backend/app/routers/run_delete.py` with `router = APIRouter(prefix="/api/test-runs", tags=["test-runs"])` and `DELETE /{run_id}` (`summary="Delete a finished test run"`, `status_code=204`) taking `current_user: User = Depends(get_current_user)` (from `app.core.auth`) and `db`, calling `delete_run(db, artifacts_router.storage, current_user.customer_id, run_id)` (`from app.routers import artifacts as artifacts_router`, read `artifacts_router.storage` at call time so tests can monkeypatch it) and answering 404 `Test run not found` for `not_found` and 409 `Cannot delete a run that is still active` for `active`.
